# R975, Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen, 28 Dec 1676

## Reading

⟨... y ya he visitado yo a los segundos y buscado el primero.⟩ Fieramente aprietan los holandeses para la paz, y nada
encubren que conduzca a este fin, y no se dan menos a entender contra nuestra desidia y desprevencion. Es menester
confesar que son buenos [?suecos], en que no menos concurren los ingleses. Las disculpas que da el conde Kinsky de una
ciatica que le ha sobrevenido en Colonia las desprecian. No obstante, se han conformado en que no hagamos extradicion
de nuestras plenipotencias hasta que llegue un ministro del señor Emperador, que no ha sido poco que ayan conocido
que era envarazar mas presentar las de los aliados uno a uno que todos juntos; pero es cierto que, proponiendo juntos
todos los reparos, el expediente que se tomare sera mas efectivo.

Han entregado a los mediadores franceses y holandeses sus plenipotencias. En la de Holanda hubo un pequeño reparo,
que ya los Estados han mandado deshacer. En las de Suecia no se puede tolerar la [?altivez] y el declarar que han
sido provocados a la guerra por haberse infringido la paz de Westfalia; semejantes clausulas de arrogancia han
arrojado franceses. Pero las dos que en su plenipotencia parecen intolerables son: la una, el decir el Rey de Francia
que ha condescendido en enviar sus embajadores al congreso por los expedientes que el Rey de Inglaterra le ha
subministrado, y espera que conseguira para poner en libertad al principe Guillermo de Furstenverg, lo cual de
ninguna manera consentira el señor Emperador; la otra la ponen los holandeses y todos los principes protestantes, que
es la que contiene la mediacion del Papa, diciendo que el instrumento de las plenipotencias es el fundamento de la
firmeza del contrato que se ha de hacer de la paz, y que este no ha de tener clausula que contradiga a la religion,
que es la vasa del derecho publico del Estado. Y con efecto, cuando los diputados en La Haya vinieron en quitar
aquella palabra "separada" en sus plenipotencias, resolvieron al mismo tiempo que no se consintiesse la expresion de
la mediacion del Papa. Los franceses respondieron que no han de alterar el contexto referido, que tiene aprovacion de
su amo; pero dicen tambien que esperan dentro de tres o cuatro dias respuesta a otra carta que han escrito; y si,
como dicen, quieren entrar derechos y obviar los inconvenientes al tratado de la paz, no dejaran de hallarse medios
terminos para esto.

Antes de anoche me dijeron los holandeses que los mediadores instaban mucho en que se permitiesen los correos
ordinarios para Suecia; y aunque a mi me icieron la salva de que justamente Su Magestad danesa lo contradecia por no
haber querido conceder esto mismo en su pasaporte, no obstante alzavan el grito por su comercio, que estava
envarazado por no poder tener correspondencias los mercaderes para las letras de cambio y los avisos. Para vencer
este punto nos ofrecen los mediadores que Francia nos permitira el libre despacho de los correos, aunque con algun
rodeo; ofrecimiento que yo no he querido responder positivamente, pero siempre nos estara vien tener libre el paso,
aunque tambien creen que, si le ponen con condiciones, ha de ser con las mismas que nosotros les concedieremos.

De la retirada del ministro de Munster, y de la disculpa de no querer tratar por esto el de Wolfenbuttel, se infiere
lo que V.S. discurre de que van de acuerdo; y yo creo que tiraran a que se trayga al congreso general esta materia,
porque deven de conprehender que han de ser mas asistidos que otros de holandeses, y que estos no tienen gran gana de
que queden con ventajas Dinamarca y Brandeburgo en el ducado de Bremen, pues mas aprisa querran conponer a Suecia de
los despojos destos principes que de los de Cel y Munster.

Aqui no parece ministro alguno de estos principes ni de Lorena, porque el Rey de Francia vien da a entender que no
esta en animo de concederles el jus legationis. Y aunque a Lorena le trata con mas decencia, y se conforma en que,
haviendo ejemplar de haberse cuvierto algun embajador del Duque, concedera los pasaportes en la misma forma, y
haviendole presentado dos, dice aora que los justifiquen, quando el Rey de Francia ha arrancado hasta el ultimo los
archivos de Nancy; a los principes del Imperio dice que desea saver lo que haran el Emperador y España, pero no se
remite a lo que estos hicieren. Yo quisiera que en esto se tomara expediente, porque estoy previniendo que el mismo
dia que se ajuste el contexto de las plenipotencias hemos de entrar en los puntos principales de hacer proposiciones,
y fuera de gran daño el que para entonces no se hallasen aqui ministros de Lorena, Zell y Munster; y en esta materia
no oso hablar, porque en Madrid no han respondido a este particular, y ya save V.S. que en nuestra corte ninguno se
cubre sino es que sea de caveza coronada, o de las republicas de Venecia o Holanda. ⟨V.S. perdone la prolijidad de
esta carta ...⟩

## Notes

- The margin decipherment runs beside the cipher on all six cipher pages (images 5049-5054) and continues across
  the full width at the foot of 5049-5053; transcribed from the images and complete. DECODE's margin text corrected
  throughout (e.g. "fieramente aprietan", "conduzca a este fin", "contra nuestra desidia", "Quinsqui", "las
  desprecian, no obstante", "extradición", "reparos", "efectivo", "semejantes cláusulas", "Fürstenberg", "protestantes",
  "la basa del derecho público del Estado", "salva de que justamente S.M. danesa", "ofrecimiento", "Wolfenbüttel",
  "jus legationis", "justifiquen", "archivos de Nancy", "se ajuste").
- The key decode (91.3% of groups in the key) agrees with the margin sentence by sentence; no figure misreads needed
  to be fixed to follow the margin, so no txi/ transcription was written.
- Code values confirmed or fixed from the margin (none entered in keyx.py): `cil` = holande- (8), `cap` = ingl-
  (ingleses), `112` = conde, `F` / `F_` = todo(s), `cep` = junt- (juntos, 2), `34` = ex (extradición, expediente,
  contexto; 4), `bi` = efect- (efectivo, efecto), `139` = plenipotencia (6), `be` = estado (2), `es` = ja/je (semejantes,
  arrojado, dijeron, ventajas, ejemplar; 5), `12` = c (declarar, escrito), `135` = parece (2: "parecen intolerables",
  "no parece ministro"; earlier letters proposed 135 = rey, which does not fit here), `154` = Rey de Francia (4), `155`
  = Rey de Inglaterra, `121` = congreso (2), `bes` = espera, `for` + ▽ = ninguna, `fos` = alguno/ninguno (2), `dol` =
  medi- (mediadores, mediación, medios; 6), `160` = respond- (respondieron, respondido), `116` = carta, `123` =
  convenien-, `dal` = mucho, `cul` = insta-, `126` = correo (2), `fil` = ordinario, `168` = Su Majestad, `136` =
  poder, `118` = correspond-, `fin` = ofrece, `ur` = despacho, `186` = ofrecimiento, `100` = algún, `dos` = Münster
  (3), `cu` = general, `143` = porque (3), `or` = Dinamarca, `bil` = forma (2), `K yu` = jus, `ig` = hi (hicieren),
  `90` = ajuste, `140` = particular, `157` = república, `f_` = Venecia, `in` = España, `des` = ministro, `Cox` = antes
  (?), `et` = noche (?); `yex` before "entrar" ("hemos de [yex] entrar") is unexplained, probably a null.
- "buenos [?suecos]": figures bue-no-s su-a-cos, margin "buenos suecos" (i.e. partial to Sweden); kept as uncertain.
- "[?altivez]": figures a-l-ti-[15]-..., margin "la altiver"; 15 unidentified.
- "han arrojado franceses": both margin and figures (a-rr-o-ja-do); the sense (the French have thrown in such
  clauses) is as written.
- Kinsky (the imperial plenipotentiary, delayed at Cologne); Wilhelm von Fürstenberg, held by the Emperor since 1674;
  Zell = Celle (Brunswick-Lüneburg), Wolfenbüttel; the Duke of Lorraine's envoys could not be admitted because France
  refused them the right of legation.

# R975, Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen, 28 Dec 1676

⟨... and I have already visited the second and sought out the first.⟩ The Dutch are pressing fiercely for peace, and
hide nothing that leads to this end, and no less do they make plain their feeling against our negligence and
unpreparedness. One must confess that they are good [?Swedes], in which the English concur no less. The excuses
Count Kinsky gives of a sciatica that has come upon him in Cologne they scorn. Nevertheless they have agreed that we
should not hand over our full powers until a minister of the Lord Emperor arrives; and it is no small thing that
they have understood that it would cause more trouble to present those of the allies one by one than all together;
but it is certain that, by putting forward all the objections together, the course that is taken will be more
effective.

They have handed their full powers to the French and Dutch mediators. In that of Holland there was a small
objection, which the States have already ordered removed. In those of Sweden the [?haughtiness] cannot be tolerated,
nor the declaring that they were provoked into the war by the breach of the Peace of Westphalia; the French have
thrown in similar clauses of arrogance. But the two that seem intolerable in their full power are: the one, the King
of France saying that he has condescended to send his ambassadors to the congress through the expedients the King of
England has supplied him, and hopes he will obtain the release of Prince Wilhelm of Fürstenberg, which the Lord
Emperor will in no way consent to; the other is raised by the Dutch and all the Protestant princes, namely the one
containing the mediation of the Pope, saying that the instrument of the full powers is the foundation of the
firmness of the contract of peace to be made, and that it must not contain a clause contradicting religion, which is
the basis of the public law of the State. And indeed, when the deputies at The Hague agreed to remove that word
"separate" in their full powers, they resolved at the same time that the mention of the Pope's mediation should not
be allowed. The French answered that they will not alter the said text, which has their master's approval; but they
also say they expect within three or four days an answer to another letter they have written; and if, as they say,
they mean to deal straightforwardly and remove the obstacles to the peace treaty, middle ways will not fail to be
found for this.

The night before last the Dutch told me that the mediators were pressing hard for the ordinary posts to Sweden to
be allowed; and although they made me the excuse that His Danish Majesty rightly opposed it for not having been
willing to grant this very thing in his passport, nevertheless they raised an outcry over their trade, which was
hampered by the merchants being unable to keep correspondence for bills of exchange and news. To overcome this point
the mediators offer us that France will allow us the free dispatch of the posts, though by a roundabout way; an
offer I have not wished to answer positively, but it will always suit us to have the passage free, although they
also believe that if they attach conditions, they will be the same ones we grant them.

From the withdrawal of the minister of Münster, and the excuse of the Wolfenbüttel one for not wishing to negotiate
on that account, one infers what Your Lordship reasons, that they act in concert; and I believe they will aim at
having this matter brought before the general congress, because they must understand that they are to be helped by
the Dutch more than others, and that the Dutch have no great wish that Denmark and Brandenburg should keep advantages
in the duchy of Bremen, since they would sooner compensate Sweden from the spoils of these princes than from those of
Celle and Münster.

No minister of these princes, nor of Lorraine, appears here, because the King of France makes it well understood
that he is not minded to grant them the jus legationis. And although he treats Lorraine with more decency, and agrees
that, there being a precedent of some ambassador of the Duke having been covered, he will grant the passports in the
same form; and two having been presented to him, he now says they should justify them, when the King of France has
carried off to the last item the archives of Nancy; to the princes of the Empire he says he wishes to know what the
Emperor and Spain will do, but he does not defer to what they may do. I should wish some course were taken in this,
for I foresee that on the very day the text of the full powers is settled we shall have to enter on the main points
of making proposals, and it would be of great harm if by then ministers of Lorraine, Celle and Münster were not here;
and on this matter I dare not speak, because in Madrid they have not answered on this particular, and Your Lordship
knows that at our court no one is covered unless he be of a crowned head, or of the republics of Venice or Holland.
⟨Your Lordship forgive the length of this letter ...⟩

R976, Ronquillo, 10 Apr 1677

Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen 10 Apr 1677: the negotiations worsen; the Dutch handed the mediators new full powers; rumours of separate Spanish talks and royal marriages worry the allies; Saint-Omer and Cambrai expected as the first article of an armistice; the Brunswick guarantee; Hanover to mediate a separate treaty with Sweden and Denmark.

<!-- Reading of the ciphered passages, built 22 Oct 2026 by Claude Opus 5.5 (ronq batch): the key decode (read/R976.dec.txt, keyx.py, on the image re-transcription txi/R976.txt) aligned word by word with the contemporary decipherment in the left margin, transcribed from the DECODE images (DECODE's margin transcription checked against them). # R976, Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen, 10 Apr 1677

## Reading

⟨... aunque no el gusto, porque cada dia⟩ van enpeorando estas negociaciones, aun mas arrebatadamente de lo que se alcanzan
nuestras desgracias en Flandes, de que no quiero repetir a V.S. congojas. Y yo reconozco que estos holandeses quieren
ya llegar a las [?treguas], pues ayer enviaron a los mediadores la nueva plenipotencia, no obstante que [...] todos
los aliados. Se podia esperar hasta el miercoles, que cumplen los sesenta dias del termino, y que las presentasen
juntamente con las del señor Emperador, Dinamarca y Brandeburgo, que an llegado; y la excusa que oy me an dado,
sobre no ser de gran sustancia, no es muy cortes.

Y estas voces que andan de que tratamos derechamente en Inglaterra y en Holanda preocupan los animos de los aliados,
y las de nuevos casamientos del Rey nuestro señor ponen en poca satisfaccion a los ministros cesareos. Yo prevengo
que la rendicion de Saint-Omer, despues de la de Canbray, sera el esencial articulo y el primero del ajustamiento de
la [?satisfaccion] o del establecimiento de un armisticio.

Ya corre la reciproca garantia de la casa de Brunswick para sus conquistas, cuarteles de invierno y defensa de las
villas de Hamburgo y Lubeck. Y no faltan noticias de que Hanover se va derechamente a las cortes de Suecia y
Dinamarca a mediar un tratado separado, y puede ser que Brandeburgo siga, sin que hasta aora ayan querido
manifestar los ministros de estos principes el nuevo [...] que an hecho sus amos, el cual, si V.S. le tiene, le
suplico me envie copia.

## Notes

- Margin and key decode agree throughout; the margin on 5056 and 5057 is complete and is read from the images, the
  short margin on 5058 is partly blotted and the cipher carries it.
- "las [?treguas]": the margin word is under an ink blot (…aras or …uas); the cipher has an unclear group then -ga-s.
- "no obstante que [...] todos los aliados": the margin verb is blotted and the cipher groups there (a tall sign, 33,
  h, 10, 40) do not give a word.
- 139 is glossed "plenipotencia" in the margin on 5056 ("la nueva plenipotencia") and "sat.n" on 5057 ("del
  ajustamiento de la sat.n"); the second is read as [?satisfacción] and the conflict is left open.
- "el nuevo [...] que han hecho sus amos": the margin has a gap there too; the cipher writes nuevo and one sign.
- Code words confirmed by the margin: dun = nuev- (nueva, nuevos, nuevo; also R984), or = Dinamarca (2), 105 =
  Brandeburgo (2), cil = holande-, 180 = satisfacci-, des = ministro, fes = noticia, up = Emperador, 167 = Suecia,
  184 = sobre, T = ta in "tratado". The leading 493 and the sign before "ya corre" carry no word.
- Cambrai and Saint-Omer were under French siege in April 1677; the sentence is about what the French would make the
  first article of a settlement.
Translation

R976_en.md

<!-- English translation of read/R976_reading.md (Claude Opus 5.5, 22 Oct 2026). Words in ⟨ ⟩ are in clear in the letter. -->

# R976, Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen, 10 Apr 1677

⟨... though not my spirits, because every day⟩ these negotiations get worse, even faster than our misfortunes in
Flanders follow one another, whose griefs I do not want to repeat to Your Lordship. And I see that these Dutch now want to
come to the [?truces], for yesterday they sent the mediators the new full powers, although all the allies [...]. One
might have waited until Wednesday, when the sixty days of the term run out, and presented them together with those of
the Emperor, Denmark and Brandenburg, which have arrived; and the excuse they gave me today, that it was of no great
substance, is not very courteous.

And these rumours going round that we are negotiating directly in England and in Holland worry the allies, and those
of new marriages of the King our master leave the Imperial ministers little satisfied. I foresee that the surrender of
Saint-Omer, after that of Cambrai, will be the essential article and the first of the settlement of the
[?satisfaction] or of the establishment of an armistice.

The mutual guarantee of the House of Brunswick for its conquests, winter quarters and the defence of the towns of
Hamburg and Lübeck is already under way. And there is no lack of news that Hanover is going straight to the courts of
Sweden and Denmark to mediate a separate treaty, and Brandenburg may follow, though so far the ministers of these
princes have not been willing to reveal the new [...] their masters have made; if Your Lordship has it, I beg you to
send me a copy.

R977, Ronquillo, 17 Apr 1677

Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen 17 Apr 1677: Windischgrätz revealed Fuenmayor's secret to the Brandenburg minister; Vienna is jealous of the allies' progress in the North and the English peace terms favour Sweden; Ronquillo had a reply sent to the Emperor before they are presented; the allies are unreliable (declaration at Celle).

Spelling (v6, 23 Oct 2026): the writer's, as the cipher gives it; the modernised spelling of 22 Oct was restored letter by letter from the key decode (read2/R977.txt) with research/reading_audit/v6_charalign.py: where the key's letters spell the same word another way they are taken (tomavan, assi, devia, aora, quando, haver), editorial accents are taken off, and ñ is kept where the key writes n (it does not distinguish them). Code words, supplied and bracketed words keep their form without accents. No word was changed. Punctuation added. Modern spelling is only in the English file. # R977, Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen, 17 Apr 1677

## Reading

⟨... y tanto apetece.⟩ Muy indiscreto ha sido el proceder del conde de Windischgratz, haviendo revelado al ministro
de Brandeburgo el secreto que V.S. le habia conferido, que ha sido el origen de nuestro [?mensaje]. Pero para que V.S.
conozca que esto no ha disminuydo la confianza con que le trato, por prueva ve los celos que motivan a la corte de
Viena los progresos de los aliados en el Norte: le dire que lo moderado de las [?paces] que el Ingles envio para hacer
a Suecos, que he alcanzado a ver, manifiestan claramente su animo de que mas quiere la vecindad de Suecia que la
altura de los principes. Pero haviendo hecho reflexion en lo perjudicioso que pudieran sernos si se llegasen a
proponer en esta conformidad, no solo por lo que irritaria a aquellos principes, sino por quedar con ellos desvanecida
la esperanza que todavia podemos tener de alguna conpensacion de las perdidas de los Paises Bajos, he conseguido que
antes de presentarla se haga una replica al señor Emperador, de que quedamos aguardando la respuesta.

Por donde podra V.S. considerar los cuidados que se nos amontonan cada dia, quando a las fatalidades y desgracias
publicas se [?fomentan] los recelos particulares de la inconstancia de nuestros aliados, cuyo animo se deja vien
conocer del papel que V.S. me remite de la declaracion que an hecho en Zell: que si fuera de sencillo animo, vien
se pudieran dar los [...] mil escudos. V.S. se sirva de no olvidar los papeles que ofrece, y continuarme las noticias
de la asanblea, y tener juntamente reservadas las que le participo. ⟨Todo lo positivo que puedo decir a V.S. en este
correo ...⟩

## Notes

- Margin decipherment on ff. 1-3 transcribed from the images; it is complete and legible except the number of
  thousands (f. 3, "dar los 8? mil es[cudos]" with a superscript) and one verb on f. 2 ("se ...an"). DECODE's margin
  transcription corrected: "indiscreto", "proceder", "Windisgratz", "revelado al ministro", "conferido", "la altura de
  los Príncipes", "sernos", "irritaría", "Payses Baxos", "réplica", "cuidados", "fatalidades", "en Zell", "sencillo",
  "ofrece", "juntamente reservadas las que le participo".
- Image check of f. 2: DECODE merged two cipher rows ("conseguido que antes de presentarla" / "se haga una réplica al
  Sr. Emperador de") into one and dropped most of the second; restored in txi/R977.txt from the image
  (`B ef ag 50 77 60 66 30 57 xa 21 8 ef 67 30 up p+` = se ha-ga u-na re-p-li-ca a-l Se-ñ-or Emperador de). Two `67`
  read on the image as `63` (= no: "sino por", "se nos"). See read/R977_corrections.tsv.
- Code values from the margin: `12` = c (3 places: indiscreto, claramente, declaración), `el` = ce (proceder), `b` = z
  (Windisgratz), `105` = Brandeburgo (key "bourg"), `175` = secreto, `ne` = fe (conferido), `con` = hecho (habiendo
  hecho; elsewhere read "haber"), `x+`/`ci` in re-f-le-xi-on, `120` = cuidados (margin; earlier letters gave 120/121 =
  congreso, which does not fit "los congresos que se nos amontonan"), `140` = particular, `es` = ja (deja), `fin` =
  ofrece, `fes` = noticia(s), `c` = ju (juntamente), `170` = Inglés (margin; another letter gave 170 = S.M. Cesárea),
  `400` = null (3 places: "que [400] pudieran", "las [400] pérdidas", "me re[400]mite"), `h+ n+` nulls in "Zell".
- `48` gives "mo" twice where the margin has "motivan" and "moderado" (elsewhere in this letter `48` = do); the
  margin is followed.
- `30` stands for r, not p, in several places in this letter (No-r-te, Se-r-or, si-r-va, re-se-r-vada, presenta-r-la);
  either a homophone or a figure DECODE reads as 30. The margin is followed in each case.
- "[?mensaje]": the cipher group after "nuestro" is a struck run (`76 40 65 a+s+ 8+`), corrected by an interlinear
  `ec af ei` (me-sa-je) above the line; the margin writes "nuestro mesaje". Uncertain.
- "las [?paces]": the group before `ye` (=es) is read 144 (= ion) by DECODE and looks like `122` on the image; the
  margin has "pazes". Uncertain.
- "se [?fomentan]": figures se-f-[703]-ta-n; the margin word is blotted. 703 unidentified.
- "los [...] mil escudos": group `225` (+ B), margin numeral unclear.
- "Brandeburgo el secreto": groups `105 138 175`; 138 is unidentified (probably el or part of the name).
- Windischgrätz is the imperial envoy; Zell = Celle (the Brunswick-Lüneburg dukes); the "asamblea" is the
  Nijmegen congress or the Danish-allied assembly reported by Fuenmayor.
Translation

R977_en.md

<!-- English translation of read/R977_reading.md (Claude Opus 5.5, 22 Oct 2026). Words in ⟨ ⟩ are in clear in the letter. -->

# R977, Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen, 17 Apr 1677

⟨... and so much desires.⟩ Very indiscreet has been the conduct of Count Windischgrätz, who revealed to the minister
of Brandenburg the secret that Your Lordship had entrusted to him, which has been the origin of our [?message]. But so
that Your Lordship may know that this has not lessened the confidence with which I treat you, as proof see the
jealousy that the progress of the allies in the North arouses at the court of Vienna: I will tell you that the
moderation of the [?peace terms] which the Englishman sent to be made to the Swedes, which I have managed to see,
plainly shows his wish that he would rather have Sweden as a neighbour than the eminence of the princes. But having
reflected on how harmful they could be to us if they came to be proposed in this form, not only for what would
irritate those princes, but because with them the hope we may still have of some compensation for the losses of the
Low Countries would vanish, I have obtained that before it is presented a reply be made to the Lord Emperor, whose
answer we are awaiting.

From this Your Lordship may consider the cares that pile up on us every day, when to the public misfortunes and
disasters are added [?fostered] the particular fears of the inconstancy of our allies, whose mind is plainly seen
from the paper Your Lordship sends me of the declaration they have made at Celle: that if it were of a sincere mind,
the [...] thousand escudos might well be given. Your Lordship be pleased not to forget the papers you offer, and to
continue to send me the news of the assembly, and also to keep reserved what I impart to you. ⟨All the positive news
I can tell Your Lordship by this post ...⟩

R978, Ronquillo, 10 Feb 1677

Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen 10 Feb 1677: the full-powers dispute (the Danish plenipotentiary insists on Latin, French or Danish); the Dutch furious at Kinsky; Ronquillo fears they will force a treaty on Spain; Windischgrätz's declaration; Orange waits on the imperial army; Lira stays in Brussels.

# R978, Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen, 10 Feb 1677 (signed for him by Bernardo Navarro)

## Reading

⟨... en que desempeñar mi obligacion.⟩ La conclusion deste diablo de proyecto de plenipotencias nos tiene cada dia
mas envarazados por las disputas y reparos que a instantes se mueven; y si vien por nuestra parte, y la de
holandeses tanvien, creo que por la del señor Emperador podia estar acavado de ajustar este punto, pues estan
conformes las minutas que hemos presentado a los mediadores, y no se halla sobre que asir, no obstante una gasconada
del plenipotenciario danes mantiene esta disputa, la cual consiste en defender y estar reacio en que, si los
franceses le entregaren sus plenipotencias en latin, entregara el la suya en la misma idioma; pero que si fuere en
frances, formara la de su amo en danes; en cuya disputa no se deja de conocer que es mucha parte de tema.

Los holandeses han entregado ya su poder firmado, y tratan de hacer lo mismo de sus pretensiones; y a esto se junta
el alvoroto que se ha levantado entre los plenipotenciarios de Holanda por haberse dejado decir el conde de Kinsky a
los mediadores que todas las minutas entregadas se habian de volver a traer a la conferencia de los aliados para que
se firmen y reconozcan los reparos que se hallan; lo cual, en la prisa de holandeses, les parece muchas largas, y
esta mañana estuvieron conmigo echando rayos y centellas, y en fin prorrumpieron en decir que no habia de haber mas
conferencia; que aunque es verdad que en todos los negocios era vien se caminase de comun acuerdo de los
confederados, que ellos no querian estar sujetos a que se les impidiese el hacer sus particulares diligencias. Y
estoy en animo de hacer las nuestras al mismo tiempo, aunque no concurran los demas, no solo por caminar unido con
esta gente, sino tambien porque, conteniendo una clausula sobre la satisfaccion de los aliados, no respondan
franceses que no hemos pedido nada.

Yo veo a estos hombres muy ansiosos por la paz; y aunque no me atrevere a afirmar que la hagan separada, por las
seguridades que cada dia me dan de que no nos avandonaran y que nos asistiran en la guerra, no obstante temo mucho
que prorrumpan en obligarnos a firmar un tratado intempestivamente, dandonos la ley que quisieren. Yo los procuro
sosegar, y me an ofrecido que, aunque retarden cuatro o cinco dias mas el presentar sus pretensiones, aguardaran a
que concurran a un mismo tiempo las nuestras; y procuro no dejarlos un punto de la mano, porque no se arrojen a mayor
desesperacion.

Repito a V.S. mi agradecimiento por las noticias que me participa de lo que ahi ocurre y por los papeles que me
remite. La declaracion que ha hecho el conde de Windischgratz de parte del señor Emperador es muy buena en lo general,
porque es dar a entender que quiere parte en las conquistas, o que los aliados acudan a garantir sus dominios de la
Alsacia; pero esta materia es menester tratarla con mucho tiento y maña, porque no nos sea de perjuicio. Los
holandeses aprobaran lo primero, porque estan en dictamen de que la parte de las conquistas contra Suecia sirvan de
permuta a nuestras perdidas en Flandes; pero lo segundo no lo arrostraran, y el señor Emperador volvera a adquirir el
credito que ha tenido de que no queria la paz y que solo mira a sus intereses particulares; y tanto mas se entrara en
este conocimiento cuanto marche por lo alto de la Mosela y se emplee en obrar por la causa comun.

Y ya V.S. habra entendido que el Principe de Orange se ha declarado en que no se movera mientras el ejercito del
señor Emperador no obrase lo proyectado en La Haya. V.S. puede considerar cual me hallare con todas estas cosas,
estando solo, y tan solo que aseguro a V.S. que ni de Madrid, de Flandes, ni de otra ninguna parte no me ayudan, ni
aun con noticias; y en medio desto se esta en Bruselas don Manuel de Lira, que era el unico que me podia ayudar desde
La Haya; pero tambien reconozco que la misma razon que justifica su ida de Holanda justifica igualmente el que no
vuelva, sin remedio; y en verdad que seria de gran conveniencia su asistencia en aquella corte. ⟨Dios nos asista, y
guarde a V.S. muchos años como deseo. Nimega 10 de febrero de 1677.⟩

## Notes

- Margin decipherment on all six cipher pages (images 5065-5070; it runs on across the page foot on 5066-5069)
  transcribed from the images; complete and legible. DECODE's margin transcription was close; corrected "proyecto de
  plenipotencias" (DECODE "prosero de Pénipotncia"), "a instantes se mueven", "creo", "están conformes", "no obstante
  una gasconada del plenipotenciario", "si los franceses le entregaren sus plenipotencias en latín, entregará él la
  suya" (missing in DECODE), "si fuere en francés", "a esto se junta", "Quinsqui", "esta mañana estuvieron",
  "prorrumpieron", "acuerdo", "sujetos", "hemos", "ansiosos", "asistirán", "temo", "prorrumpan", "un tratado
  intempestivamente", "procuro sosegar", "a que concurran a un mismo tiempo", "no se arrojen a mayor desesperación",
  "agradecimiento", "Windisgratz", "tiento y maña", "perjuicio", "permuta", "Flandes", "el S. Emp. volverá",
  "conocimiento cuanto", "hallaré", "estando solo y tan solo", "en medio desto", "Bruselas", "igualmente".
- The key decode (95.9% of groups in the key) agrees with the margin throughout; the few unread groups are code
  words, all supplied by the margin. DECODE writes the ▽ sign (= a) as "W at e d"/"W at e r"; `nue ▽` = nuestra.
- Values carried by the margin: `12` = c (conclusión, proyecto), `59` = lu, `re` = ye in pro-ye-c-to (one place;
  key gives re), `fis` = negocio (DECODE split it "f is"), `139` = plenipotencia, `bin` = franceses, `bil` = forma,
  `cil` = holande-, `112` = conde, `185` = tam(bién), `X` = ju (perjuicio), `188` = tr,
  `97` = aunque (3).
- "o que los aliados acudan a garantir sus dominios": the margin writes "o"; read as an alternative (the Emperor wants
  a share of the conquests, or an allied guarantee of his Alsatian lands).
- Date: the letter reads "Nimega 10 de febr. de 1677" (the docket on 5071 also looks like "a 10 de febr."); DECODE's
  catalogue name says 20 February. The letter was signed for Ronquillo by Bernardo Navarro, who explains in a clear
  postscript that Ronquillo was out of the house when the post had to go.

<!-- English translation of read/R978_reading.md (Claude Opus 5.5, 22 Oct 2026). Words in ⟨ ⟩ are in clear in the letter. -->

# R978, Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen, 10 Feb 1677 (signed for him by Bernardo Navarro)

⟨... in which to discharge my obligation.⟩ The settling of this devil of a draft of full powers keeps us more
embarrassed every day by the disputes and objections raised at every moment; and although on our side, and the
Dutch side too, and I believe on that of the Lord Emperor, this point could have been finished, since the drafts we
have presented to the mediators agree and there is nothing to catch hold of, nevertheless a gasconade of the Danish
plenipotentiary keeps this dispute alive. It consists in his maintaining and stubbornly insisting that, if the French
hand him their full powers in Latin, he will hand over his in the same language; but if it is in French, he will
draw up his master's in Danish; in which dispute it is plain that much of it is obstinacy.

The Dutch have already handed in their signed power and are trying to do the same with their claims; and to this is
added the uproar raised among the plenipotentiaries of Holland because Count Kinsky let slip to the mediators that
all the drafts handed in were to be brought back to the conference of the allies so that they might be signed and the
objections found be examined; which, in the Dutch hurry, seems to them a great deal of delay; and this morning they
were with me hurling thunderbolts and sparks, and finally burst out saying that there was to be no more conference;
that although it is true that in all business it was well to proceed by common accord of the confederates, they did
not wish to be bound so as to be prevented from making their own particular moves. And I am minded to make ours at
the same time, even if the others do not join, not only so as to keep step with these people, but also because, if
it contains a clause on the satisfaction of the allies, the French cannot answer that we have asked for nothing.

I see these men very eager for peace; and although I would not dare affirm that they will make it separately, given
the assurances they give me every day that they will not abandon us and will assist us in the war, nevertheless I
much fear they may break out into forcing us to sign a treaty out of season, laying down for us whatever law they
please. I try to calm them, and they have promised me that, even if it delays the presenting of their claims by four
or five more days, they will wait for ours to come in at the same time; and I try not to let them out of my hand for
a moment, lest they throw themselves into greater despair.

I repeat to Your Lordship my thanks for the news you impart of what happens there and for the papers you send me. The
declaration Count Windischgrätz has made on behalf of the Lord Emperor is very good in general, for it gives to
understand that he wants a share in the conquests, or that the allies should come to guarantee his dominions in
Alsace; but this matter must be handled with great care and skill, so that it does not harm us. The Dutch will
approve the first, because they are of the opinion that the share of the conquests against Sweden should serve as an
exchange for our losses in Flanders; but the second they will not face, and the Lord Emperor will regain the
reputation he has had of not wanting peace and of looking only to his own particular interests; and this will be the
more recognised the more he marches along the upper Moselle and is employed in working for the common cause.

And Your Lordship will already have heard that the Prince of Orange has declared that he will not move as long as
the Lord Emperor's army does not carry out what was planned at The Hague. Your Lordship may consider how I shall find
myself with all these things, being alone, and so alone that I assure Your Lordship that neither from Madrid, nor
from Flanders, nor from any other quarter do they help me, not even with news; and in the midst of this Don Manuel de
Lira, who was the only one who could help me from The Hague, is staying in Brussels; but I also recognise that the
same reason that justifies his leaving Holland equally justifies his not returning, there is no help for it; and in
truth his presence at that court would be of great advantage. ⟨God help us, and keep Your Lordship many years as I
wish. Nijmegen, 10 February 1677.⟩

R979, Ronquillo, 9 Jan 1677

Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen 9 Jan 1677: backs Fuenmayor's proposed course; the Emperor aims at Breisach and Alsace, not Flanders; the English mediators passed on the Franco-Swedish draft; the Dutch are eager for peace and Kinsky does not grasp Spain's plight.

# R979, Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen, 9 Jan 1677

## Reading

⟨... en que ejercitar mi obediencia.⟩ Veo por lo que V.S. se sirve participarme en su citada las dificultades que
tambien se ofrecen en esa conferencia; que si en la constitucion presente de nuestros negocios son dañosas las que en
este congreso impiden el llegar a tratar de los puntos esenciales de la paz antes de entrar la primavera, no lo son
menos esas para la disposicion de las operaciones de la venidera campaña. Y asi, para evitar la irresolucion, discordia
y animosidades [?que] della es provable se originen, juzgo de no poder hallarse expediente mas a proposito que el que
V.S. ha discurido; pues, si fuere posible conseguirle, nos quitaremos de todos estos embarazos, dejaremos los
negocios [?indecisos], y con las demoliciones que V.S. propone dejaremos desfortalecido un pais que, en cualesquiera
manos que este, nunca estara en buenas para nosotros.

En fin, por lo que ahi pasa y lo que aqui puedo reconocer, voy viendo cada dia con mayor evidencia que todos tiraran
unicamente a sus conveniencias propias. Y quando en la mala postura de nuestras cosas, motivada de la desprevencion
de todo lo necesario en los Flandes, de los progresos que franceses hacen en Mesina, de la declarada inpaciencia con
que holandeses desean y solicitan la paz, y del desconsuelo de los pueblos, acrecentado con el desgraciado reencuentro
de daneses, cuya restauracion temo nos ha de ser muy costosa, habia de ser el mayor fundamento de nuestras esperanzas
las fuerzas del señor Emperador y la diversion que pudieran hacer sus tropas con una entrada en Francia, por llamar
parte de las que va juntando el Cristianisimo en la frontera de Flandes y separar el golpe fatal que amenaza estos
paises indefensos, vemos que todo su fin es la recuperacion de Brisach y la Alsacia alta; y por mas colores que
quieran dar esos ministros cesareos para vorrar las justas impresiones que ha hecho su desentonada declaracion,
sienpre se trasluce el fondo de su politica, que es atraer las tropas del Norte a auxiliarles en la ejecucion de sus
enpresas particulares, y hacer la guerra sin acordarse ni de Flandes ni de su proxima ruina. El pretexto de reconocer
el animo de los principes no es satisfactorio, porque, en mi entender, ellos seran buenos como los paguen,
particularmente los de Brunswick. Pero lo que temo es que esta declaracion intempestiva de los cesareos nos sea muy
nociva aun para la admision de ese expediente, por insensiblemente que se procure inducirlos a la admision del [934].
⟨De las cosas de este congreso dire a V.S. ...⟩

⟨... se siga el formulario de cada una.⟩ Es verdad que los ingleses, cuando le comunicaron [?a] daneses y holandeses, no
dijeron era de franceses y suecos; pero habiendoles yo reconvenido con algunas razones fuertes, me confesaron
francamente la verdad, diciendo que, como mediadores, no podian excusar recivir los papeles que presentase una de las
partes y comunicarlos a las otras en el estado que [?quedan] hoy los preliminares. Dandome a mi gran cuydado el ver
la ansia con que holandeses desean la paz, lo cual me hace temer algun arevatamiento en ellos, que no seria juicio
temerario en pensarlo segun su anelo y nuestra mala postura en todas partes, que es la razon con que nos
reconvienen; no sirviendome de menor confusion la poca extension que trae el conde Kinsky, que llego a esta villa tres
dias ha, pues habiendo estado juntos mas de dos oras he reconocido que en Viena no se deve de conprehender nuestra
pesima constitucion; con que yo procuro suavizarle y darle a entender poco a poco lo que mas conviene, habiendo yo
discurido los medios para detener en lo que se pueda la velocidad con que holandeses quieren caminar en estos
negocios, con el motivo del segundo proyecto de [?plenipotencia] formado por franceses y suecos; y no omito al mismo
tiempo lo que puede conducir al mas seguro y breve reposo de la Cristiandad, con que, por lo menos, los franceses es
muy posible que quieran adelantar sus progresos en aquella parte, para reconpensar con lo que conquistaren la
conveniencia que podiamos lograr en el Pais Bajo. ⟨Guarde Dios a V.S. muchos años como deseo. Nimega, enero 9 de
1677.⟩

## Notes

- Margin decipherment on the five cipher pages (images 5072-5074, 5075 foot, 5076, 5077) transcribed from the images;
  complete and legible. DECODE's margin transcription corrected throughout, e.g. "citada", "dificultades", "dañosas",
  "puntos esenciales de la paz", "venidera campaña", "evitar la irresolución, discordia y animosidades que della",
  "juzgo de no poder hallarse expediente", "nos quitaremos de todos estos embarazos", "dejaremos desfortalecido",
  "cualesquiera manos", "todos tirarán únicamente a sus conveniencias propias", "postura", "Mesina", "acrecentado",
  "reencuentro", "temo", "costosa, había de ser", "entrada en Francia", "va juntando el Cristianísimo en la frontera",
  "indefensos; vemos que todo", "Brisac", "ministros cesáreos", "borrar", "desentonada", "trasluce el fondo de su
  política", "atraer las tropas del Norte", "satisfactorio", "Brunswick", "inducirlos", "habiéndoles yo reconvenido",
  "francamente", "arrebatamiento", "juicio temerario", "anhelo", "Quinsqui", "estado juntos", "constitución",
  "suavizarle", "detener", "Cristiandad".
- The key decode (95.5% of groups in the key) agrees with the margin sentence by sentence; no label fixes were needed.
  Code values the margin supplies: `fis` = negocio (DECODE splits it "f is"; 3), `X` = ju (juzgo, justas), `12` = c
  (Brisac, declaración, 3), `bin` = franceses, `cil` = holande- (4), `139` = plenipotencia, `123` = convenien-, `143` =
  porque, `140` = particular, `dan` = -mente.
- "admisión del [934]": the passage ends on group `934` before the clear text; the margin stops at "del". 934 is not in
  keyx.py (probably a name or "expediente"); left unread.
- "[?quedan]": figures `[b] d que dan` (b unidentified); the margin is unclear ("que le dan"?).
- "[?plenipotencia]": figures pro-y-[d]-[1]-t de e-c-[o]-r-mado; the margin abbreviates "de Ple.a formado". The clear
  text above (f. 4) says the mediators gave the Dutch and Danes a second draft of full powers in Latin and in French
  and Swedish, which supports it.
- "dejaremos los negocios [?indecisos]": margin and figures (in-de-ciso-s) agree, but the sense is odd (perhaps "los
  negocios decisos"); kept as written.
- Kinsky arrived at Nijmegen about 6 January 1677. The Danish "reencuentro" is presumably the defeat at Lund (December 1676).

# R979, Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen, 9 Jan 1677

⟨... in which to exercise my obedience.⟩ I see from what Your Lordship is pleased to impart to me in your said letter
the difficulties that also arise in that conference; that if, in the present state of our affairs, those which in
this congress prevent us from coming to treat of the essential points of the peace before spring begins are harmful,
those are no less so for the arrangement of the operations of the coming campaign. And so, to avoid the irresolution,
discord and animosities likely to arise from it, I judge that no course more to the purpose can be found than the one
Your Lordship has devised; for, if it can be obtained, we shall rid ourselves of all these embarrassments, we shall
leave the affairs [?undecided], and with the demolitions Your Lordship proposes we shall leave unfortified a country
which, in whatever hands it may be, will never be in good ones for us.

In short, from what passes there and what I can see here, I see more clearly every day that all will aim solely at
their own advantage. And when, in the bad posture of our affairs, caused by the lack of everything needful in
Flanders, by the progress the French are making at Messina, by the open impatience with which the Dutch desire and
seek peace, and by the dejection of the peoples, increased by the unfortunate encounter of the Danes, whose recovery I
fear must cost us dear, the greatest ground of our hopes should have been the forces of the Lord Emperor and the
diversion his troops could make by an entry into France, to draw off part of those the Most Christian King is
gathering on the frontier of Flanders and turn aside the fatal blow that threatens these defenceless provinces, we see
that his whole aim is the recovery of Breisach and Upper Alsace; and whatever colours those imperial ministers may
wish to give to erase the just impressions their ill-judged declaration has made, the bottom of their policy always
shows through, which is to draw the northern troops to help them carry out their own particular enterprises, and to
make war without a thought for Flanders or its approaching ruin. The pretext of sounding the mind of the princes is
not satisfactory, because in my understanding they will be good as long as they are paid, particularly those of
Brunswick. But what I fear is that this untimely declaration of the imperialists may be very harmful to us even for
the acceptance of that course, however imperceptibly one tries to induce them to accept the [934]. ⟨Of the affairs of
this congress I will tell Your Lordship ...⟩

⟨... the form of each be followed.⟩ It is true that the English, when they communicated it [?to] the Danes and the
Dutch, did not say it was from the French and Swedes; but when I took them to task with some strong arguments, they
frankly confessed the truth to me, saying that as mediators they could not avoid receiving the papers one of the
parties presented and communicating them to the others in the state in which the preliminaries [?stand] today. It
causes me great concern to see the eagerness with which the Dutch desire peace, which makes me fear some rash move on
their part, and it would be no rash judgement to think so, given their longing and our bad posture everywhere, which
is the argument they throw at us; and no less perplexing to me is the narrow brief Count Kinsky brings, who arrived in
this town three days ago, for having been together more than two hours I have seen that in Vienna they cannot grasp
our dreadful condition; so I try to soften him and to make him understand little by little what is most fitting,
having devised the means to slow down as far as possible the speed with which the Dutch wish to move in these
affairs, on the occasion of the second draft of [?full powers] drawn up by the French and Swedes; and at the same time
I do not neglect whatever may lead to the surest and speediest repose of Christendom, since at the least the French
may very possibly wish to push their progress in that quarter, to make up with what they conquer for the advantage we
could gain in the Low Countries. ⟨God keep Your Lordship many years as I wish. Nijmegen, 9 January 1677.⟩

R980, Ronquillo, 27 Jan 1677

Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen 27 Jan 1677: Brunswick and Münster pull out of the Bremen assembly; rumours of a Dutch-Swedish separate peace; the suspension-of-arms argument with Amerongen; a separate Swedish peace a precipice; Kinsky wastes time; the French show no wish for peace.

# R980, Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen, 27 Jan 1677

⟨Doy a V.S. las gracias con todo el reconocimiento que debo a las noticias que se sirve de darme de lo que ahi se
ofrece,⟩ y por ellas reconoco que los Principes de Brunswick y Obispo de Munster piensan ya de retirar la negociacion
de essa asamblea, segun las largas que interponen sus ministros en juntarse. Yo llego a creher que no solo sera por lo
que toca a la proposicion de imperiales, cuyo escozor me dice V.S. tienen muy presente, sino es mas particularmente
porque habran entendido algo de que en Holanda se trata de hacer con Suecia una paz separada, lo cual es preciso les
toque en lo mas vivo, pues reconoceran de quanto perjuicio les sera esta novedad. Y assi me afirmo mas en este dictamen,
quanto veo la publicidad con que se habla ya en la materia, que no dudo habra llegado a la noticia de V.S., pues hasta
de Inglaterra me lo escriben a mi.

Estas voces han disminuido mucho las que corrieron de suspension de armas, y el discurso que V.S. hizo sobre este punto
a Monsieur Amerongen es tan prudente que con mucha vanidad puedo asegurar me he valido de las mismas razones que V.S.
apunta en las ocasiones que se han ofrecido hablar de la materia. Queda ya, como dije a V.S., con menos fuerza; pero yo
creeria que en el estado en que estamos en Flandes, tan miserable que excuso yo el repetirle la melancolia que le
motivaran las cartas de Bruselas (que si a V.S. le sucede lo que a mi, podemos dar por muy vien empleado lo sucinto de
ellas, a trueque de que con la mayor extension no nos aumenten el desconsuelo), nos seria de gran beneficio el ajustar
una suspension de armas hasta fin de junio, y al contrario de sumo daño si hubiera de ser por cuatro años, como
discurria Amerongen, por las razones que V.S. pondera y yo conprehendo. Pero por lo mismo que nosotros
contradeciremos lo que nuestros enemigos desean, se valdrian ellos para no admitirla; y assi me parece que este punto no
podra tener ajuste, y que sera menester pensar solo en la guerra.

Y volviendo a la paz separada que holandeses desean hacer con Suecia, que aunque en el congreso no se ha llegado a
hablar abiertamente, es fijo que en La Haya se hara con grande ahinco, dire a V.S. que esta proposicion ha sido hecha
por los mismos suecos, añadiendo que en caso que el Rey de Francia no quisiese venir en ella, la ajustarian ellos por si
solos y se declararian contra el Cristianisimo. Es cierto que los holandeses anhelan como siempre por la paz con
Suecia, diciendo que no ay otro remedio para la paz general sino compensando con las perdidas de Suecia las nuestras
en el Pais Bajo; pero al mismo paso que este ajuste nos podia ser de gran conveniencia haciendose en una paz general,
nos exponemos al precipicio si la de Suecia se hace separada, pues es fijo que perderemos los aliados del norte, que el
Rey de Suecia no nos podra ayudar, y que, teniendolos tan cercanos a nuestros dominios, nos pueden hacer una mala burla
tomando el partido de Francia, particularmente en coyuntura que son tan grandes las sumas que les devemos. Con que, si
este negocio no sigue a la paz universal, encuentro muchos inconvenientes en entablarle aora; con que devo suplicar a
V.S. se sirva de participarme lo que ay entendiere, para que este prevenido quando se llegare a hablar de este
particular en el congreso.

Todo ha sido boberias y batallar en borracheras quanto hasta aora se ha tratado en el, haviendonos envarazado el
tiempo el Conde de Kinsky con sus alemanadas, muy preciado de que venia con maximas y reflexiones italianas, con que la
mayor parte del tiempo se ha gastado en conponer sus pendencias. El punto del proyecto de la plenipotencia comun esta
resuelto, y en la conferencia que tendremos mañana los aliados creo que vendra aprovado por los enemigos; y no doy parte
a V.S. de los reparos que se han ofrecido porque le enviare copia de el quando este acavado de ajustar. Solo dire a V.S.
que al paso que por nuestra parte se procura facilitar el acomodamiento de los preliminares, en que no puedo negar
enpleo mi mayor solicitud, dan a conocer franceses la poca gana que tienen de paz, cuyo dictamen le tienen ya
descuvierto; y aseguro a V.S. que todo ha sido menester para desvanecer las voces que nuestros enemigos habian
esparcido, y habian hecho comprehender en todo el mundo, que nosotros no queriamos la paz, dilatando los medios que
podian conducir a ella. Veremos lo que resulta de la conferencia de mañana, y de todo dare parte a V.S.

## Notes

- The margin decipherment runs beside every cipher line on ff. 1-6 and is written on beyond the last cipher line of
  each page across the foot of the leaf (so each page's margin ends with the first words of the next page's cipher). It
  was read from the images in full; DECODE's transcription garbles it ("Bransuse", "Jurarse", "ono remedio", "bovesias",
  "Quinsgui").
- Below the date on f. 6 a secretary adds in clear that, don Pedro being out of the house and the post leaving, he
  signs for him; f. 7 is the address leaf (Nimega 27 de enero 1677, don Pedro Ronquillo).
- f. 1, "no solo será por lo que toca a la proposición de imperiales": the margin's line is overwritten ("no solo será
  por / ha ocasionado / lo que después a la / proposición"); the figures give "por lo que t-o-[al] a la [propos]ición
  de [imperia]les", and the "no solo ... sino es más particularmente" frame of the next page decides it. "cuyo escozor
  me dice V.S. tienen": margin "cuyo escozor me dice", key "...me d i el V.S. t in en".
- "contra el Cristianísimo": margin "Xpmo" (abbreviation), key "contra l ... simo".
- "boberías y batallar en borracheras": margin "bouerias / y batallar en bora/cheras", key "bovei a sy [104] r en
  vorra[et]ras".
- Code words confirmed by the margin (not in keyx.py): `99` = armas (2), `90` = ajuste/ajustar (4), `121` = congreso (2),
  `140` = particular(mente) (3), `143` = porque (2), `cil` = holande(ses)/Holanda (3), `fa` = novedad, `dal` = mucho(s)
  (2), `fis` = negocio(ación) (2), `dol` = medio (2: remedio, medios), `116` = carta, `109` = Bruselas, `107` = beneficio,
  `154` = Rey de Francia, `182` = siempre, `139` = plenipotencia, `bu` = enemigo (3, "nuestros enemigos"), `bon` =
  fuerza, `fol` = ocasión, `123` = convenien- (2: conveniencia, inconvenientes), `104` = batalla, `cu` = general (2),
  `34` = ex (2: extensión, exponemos), `12` = c (dictamen, declararían, trueque). `135` stands where the margin has "me
  parece" ("y así [eo 135] que este punto"), not "rey". `11` stands in "re-[11]-le-xiones" (reflexiones), against R974's
  consejo: one of the two is a different figure.
- Kinsky is the Imperial plenipotentiary at Nijmegen; Amerongen the Dutch envoy in Copenhagen.

# R980, Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen, 27 Jan 1677

⟨I thank Your Lordship with all the gratitude I owe for the news you are pleased to give me of what is happening
there,⟩ and from it I see that the Princes of Brunswick and the Bishop of Münster are already thinking of withdrawing
the negotiation from that assembly, judging by the delays their ministers put in the way of meeting. I come to believe
that this will not only be on account of the proposal of the Imperialists, whose sting, Your Lordship tells me, they
keep very much in mind, but even more particularly because they must have heard something of the Dutch treating to
make a separate peace with Sweden, which must touch them to the quick, for they will see how harmful this novelty will
be to them. And I hold to this view all the more as I see the openness with which the matter is already spoken of,
which I do not doubt will have come to Your Lordship's notice, since even from England they write it to me.

This talk has much reduced the rumours that went about of a suspension of arms, and the argument Your Lordship made on
this point to Monsieur Amerongen is so prudent that I can say with much pride that I have used the same reasons Your
Lordship gives on the occasions that have arisen to speak of the matter. It now carries less weight, as I told Your
Lordship; but I would think that, in the state we are in in Flanders, so wretched that I spare myself repeating to you
the gloom that the letters from Brussels will cause you (if it is with Your Lordship as with me, we may count their
brevity well spent, so long as they do not add to our distress at greater length), it would be of great benefit to us
to agree a suspension of arms until the end of June, and on the contrary of the greatest harm if it were to be for four
years, as Amerongen argued, for the reasons Your Lordship weighs and I understand. But for the very reason that we
shall contradict what our enemies want, they would use it to refuse it; and so it seems to me that this point cannot
be settled, and that we shall have to think only of the war.

And to return to the separate peace the Dutch wish to make with Sweden: although it has not yet come to be spoken of
openly in the congress, it is certain that at The Hague it will be pursued with great insistence. I will tell Your
Lordship that this proposal was made by the Swedes themselves, adding that if the King of France would not agree to
it they would settle it by themselves and declare against the Most Christian King. It is certain that the Dutch long,
as always, for peace with Sweden, saying that there is no other remedy for a general peace than to offset our losses
in the Low Countries with Sweden's losses; but while this settlement could be very convenient for us if made within a
general peace, we expose ourselves to the precipice if Sweden's is made separately, for it is certain that we shall
lose the allies of the North, that the King of Sweden will not be able to help us, and that, having them so close to
our dominions, they can play us a nasty trick by taking the side of France, particularly at a juncture when the sums we
owe them are so great. So if this business does not go along with the universal peace, I find many drawbacks in
opening it now; and so I must beg Your Lordship to be pleased to let me know what you hear there, so that I may be
prepared when this matter comes to be discussed in the congress.

Everything that has been dealt with in it so far has been foolishness and drunken squabbling, the Count Kinsky having
taken up our time with his German ways, very proud of coming with Italian maxims and reflections, so that most of the
time has been spent in settling his quarrels. The point of the draft of the common full power is resolved, and in the
conference we allies shall hold tomorrow I believe it will come approved by the enemy; and I do not tell Your Lordship
of the objections that have been raised because I shall send you a copy of it when it is fully settled. I shall only
tell Your Lordship that, while on our side we try to ease the arrangement of the preliminaries (in which I cannot deny
I employ my greatest care), the French show how little wish they have for peace, an attitude they have already
revealed; and I assure Your Lordship that all of it was needed to dispel the rumours our enemies had spread, and had
made the whole world believe, that we did not want peace and were delaying the means that could lead to it. We shall
see what comes of tomorrow's conference, and I shall report everything to Your Lordship.

R981, Ronquillo, 4 Mar 1677

Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen 4 Mar 1677: the Swedish separate peace; Denmark and Scania; Brandenburg's claim to Pomerania and the Emperor's objection; the Celle minister's demand for ambassadorial passports; the allies' proposals ask for restoration to the state of 1665.

# R981, Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen, 4 March 1677

## Reading

⟨... y solo echo menos los empleos del servicio de V.S. para ejercitar mi obediencia.⟩ En anvas cartas discurre V.S.
muy largo sobre la paz separada con Suecia que se trata; y que se fomento por holandeses es cierto, y save Dios lo que
sera. La libertad del comercio lo es tanto que sera alivio para las tres provincias que no tengan los suecos exencion
en el norte; tambien lo es, pero no se si sera peor que la tenga Dinamarca, como sucedera si gana la Escania, que es
buen [?menajero], y mucho mas quedando dueño de anvas costas del Sund; y no se si se acomodaran mejor las provincias a
la exencion de los suecos que a la absoluta dominacion del dinamarques.

Dios nos de buen suceso en la conferencia de [?Wesel], que si en ella el Elector de Brandeburgo puede mas Stetin que
las persuasiones de los demas, es menester estar alerta. Y en amistad dire a V.S. que el Elector tiene miedo de que el
señor Emperador no le consienta el que se quede con la Pomerania, y es justo, porque mas le importa al señor Emperador
que posean aquellas provincias los suecos que no el Elector de Brandeburgo, y con razon es induvitable. Y sobre esto he
tenido algunos ratos no tan gustosos como el de una comedia, porque el domingo en la noche estavan resueltos los
brandeburgueses a pedirla derechamente en sus proposiciones, y los imperiales precisados a contradecirlo, porque se
alvorotara el Imperio si no sucediera esto, y el Emperador no lo podia consentir; porque, sea o por razon de conquista
o de feudo caduco, es interesado: si por lo primero, ya se ve que ha asistido con cuerpo de ejercito, y si por lo
segundo, que es lo mas regular, sienpre que saliese de la casa de Suecia buelve al cuerpo del Imperio y toca al
Emperador dar la investidura a quien quisiere. Con este cuidado vinieron a mi los cesareos; y teniendo yo noticia de
que todo nuestro partido estribaba en tener firme al Elector, se lo manifeste, con que tome el temperamento de ir yo a
ofrecer montes y valles a los brandeburgueses, y que el Emperador estaba por su amo, ponderandoles al mismo tiempo que
ellos mismos se perdian si pedian la Pomerania en sus proposiciones; y se ajusto el expediente de que los imperiales y
ellos la hiciesen en general, y los embajadores imperiales pasaron muy buenos oficios a este fin. Y son tantos los
enredos que an sido menester desmarañar, que tuviera mucho que decir a V.S., y por esta razon no estoy con sosiego. Es
verdad que ha habido cartas del ministro que el Elector tiene en Madrid de buena tinta y de buenos doblones, con que
deven de haberle estrenado; mostraronme oy la copia los brandeburgueses, y se me declararon con mas sinceridad en
buena forma; pero Dios sobre todo.

Ha tres dias que partio de aqui [?Mulle], ministro de Zell que reside en La Haya, y aunque estuvo incognito, a cada uno
nos hizo una reverencia con una proposicion en dos partes: la una contiene el pedir sus pasaportes con la calidad de
embajadores, y la segunda que se suspendiese la tractacion de la paz hasta que los hubiesen conseguido; que, sobre ser
desvergonzada a la varva de tan grandes reyes y tan poderosos principes, es una diligencia que hace heder ya la
neutralidad. Yo le respondi que hablase con los demas, que nuestro amo no seria el dificil; y ademas de esto no hablo de
subsidios, que para mi es mas fatal señal. Pero si Brandeburgo tiene firme, puede ser que los Brunswick y Munster se
pongan de mejor ayre, y en ese caso estare yo sosegado de que no se trata de paz separada con Suecia.

Aunque tengo hechas las extradiciones de la nueva plenipotencia con Francia, todavia con los godos nos estamos asi,
porque pretende Suecia que, como es en español la nuestra, ha de ser la suya en sueco; pero creo que aora caeran de su
burro, viendo que ya hemos entregado los aliados nuestras proposiciones, que al fin oy a mediodia quedaron en manos de
los mediadores, y presto tendremos la extradicion de las de nuestros enemigos, con que ya esta en la plaza el primer
tomo de esta negociacion. Las nuestras son las incluidas, a que las acompaña la carta que va con ellas; y despues del
incienso que era menester umar en el parlamento, se apunta la razon de pedir que seamos restituidos al estado del año
de 65. En justicia no pueden ser mas rectas, pero en la ley de las armas y en nuestra debilidad las derivara un
papirote. No he juzgado hacerlas pidiendo distintamente cada cosa de las que se encierran desde el año de 65 aca, por
no hacer mayor horror pidiendo la paz de los Pirineos, que tambien contradijera al deseo de mejorar, si se puede, el
tratado de Westfalia en favor del circulo de Borgoña, y que no se le dificulte al señor Emperador el asistirnos; y por no
tocar esta tecla, y por dejar abierta la puerta por si Dios quisiere que entre algun buen suceso por ella, se puso el
articulo sexto con aquel exordio de los daños que habia mostrado la experiencia. El segundo es tan de tabla el ponerse
en las proposiciones como el no concederse en los tratados; lo mismo digo del cuarto y del quinto, pero servira esta
espanpanata para nuestros flamencos. Como el caso de Mesina es de diversa naturaleza, se pone en articulo separado. El
ultimo es de los aliados, que es el "aqui paz y despues gloria": Dios nos de quanto antes lo primero y despues lo
segundo.

## Notes

- R980 and R981 are two different letters, not one letter held twice: the images differ on every page, and R981 is
  dated in clear on f. 7 "Nimega y marzo 4 de 1677" (the figure may also be read 9; DECODE has 4), with a different
  opening ("Los embarazos de estos días no me han permitido el responder antes a la carta de V.S. de 19 del pasado"). The
  batch list's 27 Jan 1677 for R981 is wrong.
- The margin decipherment runs beside every cipher line on ff. 1-6 and across the foot of each page, ending on f. 7
  beside the last cipher line ("cuanto antes lo primero y después lo segundo"). Read from the images in full; DECODE's
  transcription garbles it ("alconia", "doncina", "Bransiques", "panpanata", "Maonfalia").
- The key decode agrees with the margin wherever it is legible: "lo es tanto que sera alivio para las tres provincias",
  "el elector tiene miedo de que el señor no le consienta el que se quede con la Pomerania", "a la barba de tan grandes
  reyes y tan poderosos principes es una diligencia que hace heder ya la neutralidad", "en la ley de las armas y en
  nuestra debilidad las derribara un papirote", "lo mismo digo del cuarto y del quinto". At "pongan de mejor aire" and
  "los Brunswick y Münster" (`dos`) the figures and margin agree.
- Code words confirmed by the margin (not in keyx.py): `er` = Dios (3), `100` = algún(os) (2), `173` = suceso (2), `91` =
  asistido/asistir (2), `90` = ajustó, `116` = carta(s) (2), `139` = plenipotencia, `cu` = general, `fan` = oficio(s),
  `bil` = forma, `dos` = Münster, `82` = ir, `fes` = noticia (also R980), `ug` = hu- (hubiesen; R980 hubiera), `cun` =
  hech- (hechas; R980 hecha), `al` = después (2), `dun` = nueva, `ben` = flamenco, `bu` = enemigo, `12` = c (tecla,
  rectas), `in` = España (español), `37` = una. `138` stands for "el" before Emperador / domingo in two places.
- "[?Wesel]": margin "Wesel"/"Webel", figures "u [1+6+] s el"; the place of the Brandenburg conference is not checked.
- "[?Mulle]": the margin writes the name of the Zell minister as "Mulle" (or "Mull"); figures "a que mule r".
- "buen [?menajero]": margin "buen menagero", figures "buen men a [er] [r+] r o"; French ménager, the same word as in
  R971.
- "espampanata" (Italian spampanata, bluster): margin "es-panpanata", figures "es panpanata". "Sund": margin "Sonte".
- "las extradiciones": the exchange (handing over) of the full powers and of the proposals through the mediators.

<!-- English translation of read/R981_reading.md (Claude Opus 5.5, 22 Oct 2026). Words in ⟨ ⟩ are in clear in the letter. -->

# R981, Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen, 4 March 1677

⟨... and I only miss being employed in Your Lordship's service, to exercise my obedience.⟩ In both letters Your Lordship
writes at great length on the separate peace with Sweden that is being negotiated; and that it was promoted by the Dutch
is certain, and God knows what will come of it. Freedom of trade is so much so that it will be a relief to the three
provinces that the Swedes should have no exemption in the North; it is that too, but I do not know whether it will be
worse for Denmark to have it, as will happen if she wins Scania, for she is a good [?manager], and much more so being
master of both shores of the Sound; and I do not know whether the provinces would put up better with the Swedes'
exemption than with the absolute dominion of the Dane.

God grant us good success in the conference of [?Wesel], for if in it Stettin weighs more with the Elector of
Brandenburg than the persuasions of the others, we must be on the alert. And in friendship I will tell Your Lordship
that the Elector fears that the Lord Emperor will not allow him to keep Pomerania, and rightly, because it matters more
to the Lord Emperor that the Swedes should hold those provinces than the Elector of Brandenburg, and with reason it is
beyond doubt. And over this I have had some hours not as pleasant as a comedy's, because on Sunday night the
Brandenburgers were resolved to ask for it outright in their proposals, and the Imperialists obliged to oppose it,
because the Empire would be in uproar if this did not happen, and the Emperor could not allow it; for, whether by right
of conquest or of lapsed fief, he has an interest: if the first, it is plain that he has helped with an army corps, and
if the second, which is the more regular, whenever it leaves the house of Sweden it returns to the body of the Empire
and it falls to the Emperor to give the investiture to whom he pleases. With this worry the Imperial ministers came to
me; and having learned that our whole party rested on keeping the Elector firm, I told them so, and so I took the course
of going myself to offer mountains and valleys to the Brandenburgers, and that the Emperor was on their master's side,
pointing out to them at the same time that they would ruin themselves if they asked for Pomerania in their proposals;
and the expedient was agreed that the Imperialists and they should make them in general terms, and the Imperial
ambassadors did very good offices to this end. And so many are the tangles that have had to be unravelled that I would
have much to tell Your Lordship, and for this reason I am not at ease. It is true that there have been letters, in good
ink and with good doubloons, from the minister the Elector keeps in Madrid, with which they must have made a start with
him; the Brandenburgers showed me the copy today, and they declared themselves to me with more sincerity in good form;
but God above all.

Three days ago [?Mulle], minister of Celle who resides at The Hague, left here, and although he was incognito he made
each of us a bow with a proposal in two parts: the one contains the request for their passports with the rank of
ambassadors, and the second that the treating of the peace be suspended until they had obtained them; which, besides
being shameless under the beard of such great kings and such powerful princes, is a step that already makes the
neutrality stink. I answered him that he should speak to the others, that our master would not be the difficult one;
and besides this he did not speak of subsidies, which to me is a more fatal sign. But if Brandenburg holds firm, it may
be that Brunswick and Münster will put on a better air, and in that case I shall be at ease that there is no question of
a separate peace with Sweden.

Although I have made the exchanges of the new full power with France, with the Goths we are still where we were,
because Sweden claims that, as ours is in Spanish, hers must be in Swedish; but I believe they will now get off their
donkey, seeing that we allies have already handed in our proposals, which at last today at noon were left in the hands
of the mediators, and soon we shall have the handing over of those of our enemies, so that the first volume of this
negotiation is now on the market square. Ours are the enclosed, accompanied by the letter that goes with them; and
after the incense that had to be burned in the preamble, the case is made for asking that we be restored to the state
of the year '65. In justice they could not be more right, but by the law of arms and in our weakness a flick of the
finger will knock them down. I did not think fit to frame them by asking separately for each of the things included
from the year '65 until now, so as not to cause greater horror by asking for the Peace of the Pyrenees, which would also
run against the wish to improve, if possible, the Treaty of Westphalia in favour of the Burgundian Circle, and so that
the Lord Emperor should find no difficulty in assisting us; and so as not to touch that key, and to leave the door open
in case God wills some good success to enter by it, the sixth article was put in with that preamble on the damage that
experience had shown. The second is as much a matter of course to put in the proposals as not to be granted in the
treaties; I say the same of the fourth and fifth, but this bluster will serve for our Flemings. As the case of Messina
is of a different nature, it is put in a separate article. The last is the allies', which is "here peace and afterwards
glory": God give us the first as soon as possible and the second afterwards.

R982, Ronquillo, 19 Mar 1678 read in part

Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen 19 Mar 1678: after the loss of Ghent Flanders is near despair; England stays irresolute; he hopes the allies drop their quarrels to defend the Low Countries and the North; Königsmarck's incursion should press the Brunswick princes; nothing worth reporting from the congress.

Builds coherent sentences, but under the read bar of 95% of words read as sense (92% here); the holes are marked in the reading.

<!-- Reading of the ciphered passages, built 22 Oct 2026 by Claude Opus 5.5 (ronq batch): read on the images, no contemporary decipherment (none in the margins of 5093-5095); the key decode (read/R982.dec.txt, keyx.py, on the image re-transcription txi/R982.txt, second pass) checked sentence by sentence against the cipher figures. Spelling (v6, 23 Oct 2026): the writer's, as the cipher gives it; the modernised spelling of 22 Oct was restored letter by letter from the key decode (read2/R982.txt) with research/reading_audit/v6_charalign.py: where the key's letters spell the same word another way they are taken (tomavan, assi, devia, aora, quando, haver), editorial accents are taken off, and ñ is kept where the key writes n (it does not distinguish them). Code words, supplied and bracketed words keep their form without accents. No word was changed. Punctuation added. Modern spelling is only in the English file. Words in ⟨ ⟩ are written in clear in the letter and lead into the cipher; [...] = hole; [?word] = uncertain; [al] = unidentified code group. -->

# R982, Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen, 19 Mar 1678

## Reading

⟨... con que me continuan los favores que V.S. me hace.⟩ [...] He visto con particular gusto los discursos de V.S. y
lo que [...] caminando acia los pasos, que son muy propios de la prudencia, celo y [?actividad] de V.S. Y pluguiera a
Dios que nuestras desgracias no fueran tan superiores [...] nos hallasemos en estado de poder [...]; pero siendo de la
mala calidad que V.S. [?habra] entendido, es menester conformarnos con la voluntad de Dios y atribuir a sus justos
juicios el que Flandes corra [...] a la ultima desesperacion, lo que, [?aseguro] a V.S., nos tiene a todos [?tan]
atonitos y sorprendidos que hasta el gusto de discurir con los [...] nos falta, [...] el dolor y consideraciones de
nuestra infelicidad.

La Inglaterra se mantiene todavia en la misma [?ambigüedad] e irresolucion que experimentamos dos meses ha, como V.S.
estara informado por el [...]; con que, reconociendo el punto critico [...] el estado presente de las cosas y el
peligro y evidente riesgo en que se hallan los Paises Bajos, y por consiguiente los demas del Norte, despues [?de] la
perdida de Gante, podemos esperar, si Dios por su misericordia no permite que experimentemos el ultimo castigo, que
nuestros aliados, excusando sus particulares controversias, se apliquen enteramente a defendernos y a defenderse todos
contra los progresos de Francia. Para lo cual, y para que los Brunswick no persistan en [...] de sus pretensiones,
obligara tambien mucho las noticias que tienen [bc]s de la entrada que ha hecho el conde de Konigsmarck en el
[?Mecklenburg]; juntandose a esta mala [...] de cosas los discursos que, por otras correspondencias habra entendido
V.S., hacen ya los [al]eses, que no pueden dejar de concevir pesimas consecuencias. Con que de todas maneras estamos
muy mal, pues [...]. Yo refiero a V.S. estas melancolicas reflexiones no solo por ser propias de [...] sino [...],
porque, haviendose [?arrogado] a las cortes de [...], La Haya y [109] la [...] de estas manos, no se [fin] que decir a
V.S. del congreso.

## Notes

- No margin decipherment on any page; the reading rests on the key and the image only. The scan is soft and the
  numerals of this hand (4, 7, 9 with flourishes) are hard to tell apart at the DECODE resolution; that, not the key,
  is what leaves most holes.
- The values used are the key's, with the code words that the sibling letters of this volume confirm: be = estado
  (estado presente de las cosas; R972 "en estado de entrar en guerra"), er = Dios (three), fes = noticia, cun = hech-,
  112 = conde, 143 = porque, 141 = para que, and two tall signs: the underlined k-like sign = tod- (todos, todas,
  todavía) and another, joined to yu or 33, = ju (justos juicios, juntándose). The underlined sign before "de Dios" is
  read as voluntad.
- Uncertain readings: "actividad" (the cipher has six groups ending -vi-da-d), "ambigüedad" (a-?-bi-gü-e-?-d), "habrá
  entendido" (a-n-d-ra en-a-n-di-do), "aseguro" (only gu-ro clear), "Mecklenburg" (me-c-le-de-bur-g), "arrogado"
  (a-b-r-ro-ga-do).
- Holes: the opening groups (M 182 491); "lo que [...] caminando"; the end of the "superiores" clause and "poder [...]";
  "corra [...]"; "discurrir con los [...]" and the words before "el dolor"; "por el [...]" (dul + de bu-r-go-ma-...);
  the person or place after "en el" in "persistan en [...]"; "mala [...] de cosas"; "pues [...]"; "propias de [...]
  sino [...]"; "cortes de [...]"; "la [...] de estas manos". 109 is probably Madrid (R980 "las cartas de [109]"), al
  probably a nation (ingleses or holandeses), fin probably "más"; none is confirmed.
- Context: Ghent fell to Louis XIV on 9-12 March 1678; Königsmarck commanded the Swedish forces in Pomerania.
