# R982, Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen, 19 Mar 1678

⟨... with which the favours Your Lordship does me continue.⟩ [...] I have read with particular pleasure Your
Lordship's reflections and what [...] moving towards the steps, which are very much in keeping with Your Lordship's
prudence, zeal and [?energy]. And would to God our misfortunes were not so overwhelming [...] we found ourselves in a
position to [...]; but as they are of the bad kind Your Lordship [?will have] understood, we must resign ourselves to
the will of God and ascribe to His just judgements that Flanders should run [...] to the last despair, which, [?I
assure] Your Lordship, leaves us all [?so] astonished and taken by surprise that even the pleasure of discussing with
the [...] fails us, [...] the grief and the thought of our misfortune.

England still remains in the same [?ambiguity] and irresolution we experienced two months ago, as Your Lordship will
have been informed by the [...]. So, seeing the critical point [...] the present state of affairs, and the danger and
evident risk in which the Low Countries, and in consequence the rest of the North, stand after the loss of Ghent, we
may hope, unless God in His mercy lets us suffer the final punishment, that our allies, setting aside their private
quarrels, will apply themselves wholly to defending us and all of them to defending themselves against the advances of
France. To that end, and so that the Brunswick princes should not persist in [...] of their claims, the news they have
[bc] of the incursion that Count Königsmarck has made into [?Mecklenburg] will also weigh heavily; and to this bad
[...] of affairs is added the talk that, as Your Lordship will have learnt from other correspondents, the [al] are
already making, who cannot help foreseeing the worst consequences. So in every way we are in a very bad state, for
[...]. I pass these melancholy reflections on to Your Lordship not only because they belong to [...] but [...],
because, the [...] of these hands having been [?taken over] by the courts of [...], The Hague and [109], I do not know
[fin] what to tell Your Lordship about the congress.

R983, Ronquillo, 22 May 1677

Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen 22 May 1677: Fuenmayor's papers and the Bremen conference; Bremen and Verden; the Baltic squadron; the northern alliances; an army needed in Flanders after the battle of Saint-Omer (Cassel), Villahermosa with hired mules; written versus verbal answers through the mediators (Jenkins).

Transcription: DOC_R983_D2509_2509.txt

<!-- Reading of the ciphered passages, built 22 Oct 2026 by Claude Opus 5.5 (ronq batch): the key decode (read/R983.dec.txt, keyx.py) aligned word by word with the contemporary decipherment in the left margin, transcribed from the DECODE images (DECODE's margin transcription checked against them). Spelling (v6, 23 Oct 2026): the writer's, as the cipher gives it; the modernised spelling of 22 Oct was restored letter by letter from the key decode (read2/R983.txt) with research/reading_audit/v6_charalign.py: where the key's letters spell the same word another way they are taken (tomavan, assi, devia, aora, quando, haver), editorial accents are taken off, and ñ is kept where the key writes n (it does not distinguish them). # R983, Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen, 22 May 1677

## Reading

⟨... paso a suplicarle no atribuya a omision ni falta de amistad el no haber correspondido con la puntualidad que
merecen los continuados favores de V.S.,⟩ pues aqui andamos tan envarazados [...] de no hacer nada, como V.S. puede
[...] contemporizar con los [...], que aqui siguen las mismas maximas que en Bremen [...] de la Pomerania, a celos [...]
poder de Brandeburgo y principes de la [...] suecos y [...]; y lo peor es que al cavo no se adelantan los tratados, y
que prematuras declaraciones [...] que son causa de las [?perdidas] [...] toman sobre su [...]. [...] Estimo a V.S., como
devo, los papeles que se sirve de remitirme, y yo siempre me holgare que se siga el proyecto que se ha formado sobre la
separacion de essa conferencia. Y para decir a V.S. la verdad, a nosotros poco nos [?pesa] que Bremen y Verden queden de
suecos [...], como se prosiga con calor la presente [...]; lo cual unicamente depende de que nosotros [...] con
diligencia la escuadra del Baltico, cuya importancia [...] no dejamos el señor don Manuel de Lira y yo de representar
con las instancias mas vivas a España cada dia. A lo que veo, los suecos se tienen firmes en no querer ceder nada de
las conquistas, y que si hacen alguna reparacion dicen que sera a Brandeburgo, pero que ella no ha de ser en tierras ni
plazas. Yo creo que V.S. no ignora el dictamen que siempre he tenido sobre las alianzas del norte, desde el tiempo que se
estava [?negociando] los tratados, y quisiera tener nosotros un ejercito propio en Flandes, que este no ay duda daria
el movimiento a las tropas de [...] y del Principe de Orange; que si vien no tenemos que [?esperar] de uno ni otro
particularmente, despues de la batalla de San Omer, es mi opinion que se obraria diferentemente si pudiese el señor
Duque de Villahermosa salir a canpaña con mulas que no fuessen de alquiler; pero en la constitucion presente plegue a
Dios que aun estas tengamos, y sobre todo medios para darles de comer. Y ya que Dios quiso que nos hallasemos
embarcados en la guerra de Suecia, hemos menester hacer el ultimo esfuerzo para llevarla adelante a todas velas y
conservar essos aliados, no tanto por el fruto que de ellos devemos prometernos como por el daño que se nos seguiria de
que se los ganase Francia, mayormente ahora que se hallan con fuerzas y gente, y que han menester dinero para
mantenerlas o deshacerse de ellas con conveniencia.

⟨En fin, no obstante la renitencia de los adversarios, hemos presentado en 18 de este nuestras respuestas a las
primeras proposiciones ... publicandonos infractores de la Paz de Aquisgran,⟩ valiendose para atraher los ingleses a
entrar en su dictamen del [?pretexto] de que si habian de responder por escrito su justificacion al punto de la
infraccion, con la declaracion que hizo contra nosotros [?el embajador de Gran Bretaña] despues del sitio que el
Principe de Orange puso a Charleroi; en la cual bien conocian procurarian evitar ingleses el empeño, de que este recurso
no haria buen partido al Rey de Inglaterra con su parlamento, que se halla con los recelos que deben darle los
monstruosos progresos del Cristianisimo y nuestra debilidad. Y aun despues de entregadas nuestras proposiciones
insinuaron los ingleses en que se enpezase a tratar en la forma que yo mismo les he sugerido, y es que, para evitar las
asperezas que pueden llevar las proposiciones de las partes que se les entregan selladas, que mas servirian de enconar
los animos que de suavizarlos, tomasen los mediadores las respuestas vervales de unos y otros y que ellos las
pusiessen por escrito; habiendome para esto enviado a Monsieur Jenkins, tercer plenipotenciario de Inglaterra, que, como
es un devoto hereje, venia a predicarme lo que yo no quise recibir por evangelio, diciendome que todavia tenian
nuestras respuestas selladas y que se podia excusar el entregarlas. Pero viendo que se las tenia [...], y hallando que
hacia tituvear a nuestros aliados y aun a los imperiales, convencido me pregunto con muy ponderadas palabras que si
pensava yo que se haria la paz por entregarse estas respuestas; a que le volvi a replicar que [...] el Rey de Francia
[...] para que las retirase. En fin, vencimos el punto Ratman y yo, que es el unico que me secundo, de suerte que
quedan en obligacion de poner nuestras respuestas en manos de franceses; y a mi poco cuidado me da el que ellos hablen
por escrito o que dejen de hacerlo, ni tanpoco la agudeza de querer empeñar al Rey de Inglaterra en la culpa que nos
echan de haber contravenido a las paces, pues espero que Dios no nos faltara en responderles decentemente sin ofender
al medianero. Ahora quedamos tratando sobre la forma en que hemos de correr en adelante, de suerte que quedemos
resguardados con nuestros amos.

## Notes

- Date and place in clear on f. 8: "Nimega 22 de mayo de 1677"; f. 7 continues in clear (Bevilacqua's passports,
  Balbases expected at Cologne).
- The margin decipherment runs beside every cipher line on ff. 2-7 and was read from the images (DECODE's transcription
  of it is mostly asterisks). On f. 1 there is a margin beside the cipher too, but it is faded nearly to nothing on
  the scan; enhanced crops give only scattered letters ("andamos", "contemporizar"?). The f. 1 passage therefore rests on
  the key decode alone and is full of holes: groups not in keyx.py (`l`, `c`, `1q`, `+5ig`, `L2`, `pA`, `11`) and the
  DECODE-split groups (`c e l` = cel = hacer, `1 7` = 17 = o). Blocker: illegible margin; key gaps on that page.
- `120` = cuidado again (f. 6, last line: "y a mí poco cuidado me da", figure 120 on the image), as in R1001 (2 places):
  three places now, against keyx.py 120 = congreso.
- The DECODE label sequence `1 7` is the single group 17 (= o): "nos-o-t-ro-s", "de O-ra-n-ge", "a-o-ra".
- `▽` ("Water") with its following `at e r` in DECODE's labelling stands inside "tratados" (2), "nuestra", "medianero";
  the clean reading comes from the margin.
- Code words confirmed by the margin (not in keyx.py): `fel` = opinión ("es mi opinión"), `bil` = forma (2: proyecto
  que se ha formado; la forma en que), `cin` = habiendo (in keyx.py), `ban` = Flandes (in keyx.py), `cun` = (h)ech-
  (f. 7 "nos echan"; R981 "hechas"), `bo` = empeño (f. 7 "empeñar"), `er` = Dios (f. 7), `167` = Suecia, `ep` = Duque, `fe` = nuestr-,
  `i`/`in` = España.
- Uncertain: "[?pesa]" (figures "[c] sa que", margin unclear), "[?negociando]", "[?esperar]", "[?pretexto]", "[?el
  embajador de Gran Bretaña]" (figures "embax contra nosotros ... reta ni ca"), and the reply to Jenkins, where the margin
  is blotted.
- Jenkins is Sir Leoline Jenkins, English mediator at Nijmegen; Ratman is presumably the Danish or Brandenburg
  plenipotentiary; the battle of "San Omer" is Cassel (11 April 1677), lost while relieving Saint-Omer; the Duke of
  Villahermosa was governor of the Spanish Netherlands.
Translation

<!-- English translation of read/R983_reading.md (Claude Opus 5.5, 22 Oct 2026). Words in ⟨ ⟩ are in clear in the letter. -->

# R983, Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen, 22 May 1677

⟨... I go on to beg you not to put down to neglect or want of friendship my not having answered with the punctuality
that Your Lordship's continued favours deserve,⟩ for here we are so hampered [...] in doing nothing, as Your Lordship can
[...] temporise with the [...], for here they follow the same maxims as in Bremen [...] of Pomerania, to the jealousy
[...] power of Brandenburg and princes of the [...] Swedes and [...]; and the worst is that in the end the treaties make
no progress, and that premature declarations [...] which are the cause of the [?losses] [...] take upon their [...].
[...] I value, as I ought, the papers Your Lordship is pleased to send me, and I shall always be glad that the plan that
has been formed on the separation of that conference should go ahead. And to tell Your Lordship the truth, it [?grieves]
us little that Bremen and Verden should remain Swedish [...], so long as the present [...] is pursued with warmth;
which depends solely on our [...] with diligence the Baltic squadron, whose importance [...] Don Manuel de Lira and I do
not cease to represent to Spain every day with the most urgent pleas. As far as I see, the Swedes stand firm in refusing
to give up any of the conquests, and say that if they make any reparation it will be to Brandenburg, but that it is
not to be in lands or fortresses. I believe Your Lordship knows the view I have always held on the northern alliances
since the time the treaties were being [?negotiated], and I would wish us to have an army of our own in Flanders, which
without doubt would set in motion the troops of [...] and of the Prince of Orange; for although we have nothing
particular to [?expect] from either, after the battle of Saint-Omer, it is my opinion that things would be done
differently if the Lord Duke of Villahermosa could take the field with mules that were not hired; but in the present
state of things God grant we have even these, and above all the means to feed them. And since God willed that we should
find ourselves embarked in the war with Sweden, we must make the last effort to carry it forward under full sail and
keep those allies, not so much for the profit we should expect from them as for the harm that would come to us if
France won them over, especially now that they find themselves with forces and men, and need money to keep them up or
to get rid of them on good terms.

⟨At last, notwithstanding the reluctance of the adversaries, we presented on the 18th of this month our answers to the
first proposals ... publishing us as infringers of the Peace of Aachen,⟩ using, to bring the English round to their
view, the [?pretext] that, if they had to answer in writing, their justification on the point of the infringement
[would rest] on the declaration that [?the ambassador of Great Britain] made against us after the siege the Prince of
Orange laid to Charleroi; a commitment which they well knew the English would try to avoid, since this recourse would
not serve the King of England well with his Parliament, which is full of the misgivings that the monstrous progress of
the Most Christian King and our weakness must give it. And even after our proposals had been handed in, the English
suggested that the treating should begin in the form that I myself had proposed to them, namely that, to avoid the
harshness the parties' proposals may carry when handed over sealed, which would serve rather to embitter minds than to
soften them, the mediators should take the verbal answers of both sides and put them in writing themselves; and for
this they sent me Monsieur Jenkins, third plenipotentiary of England, who, being a devout heretic, came to preach to me
what I would not receive as gospel, telling me that they still held our answers sealed and that handing them over
could be dispensed with. But seeing that I held to it [...], and finding that he was making our allies and even the
Imperialists waver, he asked me, convinced, with very weighty words, whether I thought peace would be made by handing
over these answers; to which I replied again that [...] the King of France [...] so that he should withdraw them. In the
end Ratman and I, who alone supported me, carried the point, so that they are now obliged to put our answers into the
hands of the French; and it matters little to me whether they speak in writing or not, nor the cleverness of trying to
involve the King of England in the blame they lay on us for having broken the peace treaties, for I trust that God will
not fail us in answering them decently without offending the mediator. Now we are discussing the form in which we are
to proceed from here on, so that we remain covered with our masters.

R984, Ronquillo, 26 Feb 1678

Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen 26 Feb 1678: on the assembly of the northern allies at Copenhagen; everything hangs on England's resolution; praises Fuenmayor for stopping the allies' ministers threatening the States General; the allies complain of Spanish subsidies paid in monthly instalments; a Hanover intrigue (a marriage to the Duchess of Celle's daughter; Estrades dealing with Hanover).

Transcription

<!-- Reading of the ciphered passages, built 22 Oct 2026 by Claude Opus 5.5 (ronq batch): read on the images, no contemporary decipherment (none in the margins of 5105-5108); the key decode (read/R984.dec.txt, keyx.py) checked sentence by sentence against the cipher figures on full-resolution crops. Spelling (v6, 23 Oct 2026): the writer's, as the cipher gives it; the modernised spelling of 22 Oct was restored letter by letter from the key decode (read2/R984.txt) with research/reading_audit/v6_charalign.py: where the key's letters spell the same word another way they are taken (tomavan, assi, devia, aora, quando, haver), editorial accents are taken off, and ñ is kept where the key writes n (it does not distinguish them). Code words, supplied and bracketed words keep their form without accents. No word was changed. Punctuation added. Modern spelling is only in the English file. Words in ⟨ ⟩ are written in clear in the letter and lead into the cipher; [...] = hole; [?word] = uncertain; [91] = unidentified code group. -->

# R984, Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen, 26 Feb 1678

## Reading

⟨... con las cuales suplico a V.S. me favorezca.⟩ He visto con particular atencion quanto V.S. me participa en sus
referidas cartas en lo tocante a las materias que se an tratado en esa asamblea de los aliados del Norte y pasos
que en ella se han dado; y pendiendo todo de las resoluciones de Inglaterra, de que V.S. se hallara informado con
anticipacion por las cartas de La Haya, devemos esperar que las cosas muden de senblante en todas partes, si las que
se atienden de aquella corte fueren de rompimiento contra Francia. Pero yo, por el conocimiento que he adquirido de
aquel reino y parlamento, y saviendo que el Rey es dueño absoluto de el y de todo lo demas, [?no] me atrevere a hacer
todavia juicio fijo ni de la paz ni de la guerra mientras no tuvieremos apariencias mas ciertas y seguras de las
deliveraciones de Su Magestad Britanica, aunque [135] que toda la disposicion es de guerra, y que vemos al Parlamento en
animo de enpeñar al Rey en ella.

V.S. obro muy cuerdamente y muy conforme a su [138] en haber procurado envarazar el que los ministros de los aliados
que [91]n en esa [113] echasen a los Estados Generales la ronca que tenian resuelto. Pudiera ser que, si lo hubieran puesto
en ejecucion, la hubiesen segundado holandeses en tal forma que les hubiese escocido muy vien, como sucedio con el otro
enviado de Dinamarca, de que V.S. estara informado. Y yo prometo a V.S. que, si llegare el caso de que ronpa el Rey de
Inglaterra, sera muy posible que amansen la colera y el paso en echar roncas, pues si se hallan solos, y el Rey de
Suecia en la Pomerania, como lo estara, no haran poco en defenderse y mirar por si, sin interponer cada dia [dun]s
novedades. Aca padecemos con ellos tambien hartas mortificaciones y sufrimos hartas inpertinencias, no dandose por
satisfechos de las remesas que an llegado de España para sus amos, particularmente el [460] danes, pasando a decir que
no pueden hacer nada con ellos respecto de haber venido en mesadas. Con que en todas partes nos dan que hacer estos
[cus]s, y en medio de que [?acabemos] con [...], en quanto al punto principal de la negociacion del congreso, es harto
considerable el trabajo que nos causa el haberlos de oir, mantener y [90]r.

Hame hecho V.S. particular favor en motivarme la intriga del Duque de Hanover y la voda del conde de [...] con [?hija]
de la Duquesa de Zel, porque ha mas de dos meses que este sujeto se halla [?aqui], que pretestando su detencion con
decir que no podia tener enpleo en la guerra respecto de la religion; y con lo que V.S. me participa podre echar mis
redes, pues, como dije a V.S., algunos dias ha tenia entendido que el Mariscal de Estrades [?trataba] con el de
Hanover por via del parentesco que tiene con Madama de [?Arvor]; y [...] que de oy en ocho dias podre comunicar a
V.S. algo fijo de esta trama.

## Notes

- No margin decipherment on any page; the reading rests on the key and the image only.
- The opening signs (149 before "he visto", and the sign before "hame hecho") and a sign like a double o after "del
  Norte" carry no word and are left out.
- "[?no] me atreveré": the cipher has the group for "i" (or "ri"); the sentence (ni ... ni ... mientras no) needs a
  negative, so "no" is only half carried.
- Code words were fixed from context and repeated use (tod-, rey, particular, haber, hech-, forma, holande-, Su
  Majestad, porque, and two nulls) and from the margined siblings read in this batch (aunque, novedad, medio,
  congreso, ir in "oír", conde, hija, carta, España, Dinamarca, general, estado); about twenty code groups stay unread, each used once or twice with nothing in
  the letter or its siblings to confirm a value. The lists are in R984_reread_log.md.
- "de hoy en ocho días": the cipher writes "o-y en 8" and a sign; the 8 is read as the numeral.
- Context: Ronquillo was Spanish plenipotentiary at the Nijmegen congress; Fuenmayor, Spanish envoy in Copenhagen,
  had reported on the meeting of the northern allies there. Estrades was one of the French plenipotentiaries at
  Nijmegen; the Duke of Hanover (Johann Friedrich) was pro-French; the Duchess of Zell is Eleonore d'Olbreuse.

<!-- English translation of read/R984_reading.md (Claude Opus 5.5, 22 Oct 2026). Words in ⟨ ⟩ are in clear in the letter. -->

# R984, Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen, 26 Feb 1678

⟨... with which I beg Your Lordship to favour me.⟩ I have read with particular attention all that Your Lordship tells
me in your said letters about the matters treated in that assembly of the northern allies and the steps taken in
it. And since everything hangs on the resolutions of England, of which Your Lordship will have been informed in
advance by the letters from The Hague, we must expect things to change their aspect everywhere, if those awaited
from that court are for a break with France. But I, from the knowledge I have gained of that kingdom and parliament,
and knowing that the King is absolute master of it and of everything else, [?shall not] yet venture any firm judgement
either of peace or of war until we have surer and more certain signs of the deliberations of His Britannic Majesty,
although [135] that the whole disposition is for war, and that we see Parliament minded to engage the King in it.

Your Lordship acted very wisely, and very much in keeping with your [138], in trying to prevent the ministers of the
allies who [91] at that [113] from making the threat they had resolved on to the States General. It might be that, had they
carried it out, the Dutch would have seconded it in such a way that it would have stung them well, as happened with
the other envoy of Denmark, of which Your Lordship will be informed. And I promise Your Lordship that, if it comes to the
King of England breaking, they may very well tame their anger and their eagerness to threaten, for if they find
themselves alone, with the King of Sweden in Pomerania as he will be, they will do well enough to defend themselves
and look to their own safety, without putting forward [dun] novelties every day. Here we too suffer much
mortification from them and bear much impertinence, as they do not declare themselves satisfied with the remittances
that have come from Spain for their masters, particularly the Danish [460], who goes so far as to say that they can do
nothing with them because they have come in monthly instalments. So everywhere these [cus] give us trouble, and while
we [?finish] with [...] as regards the main point of the negotiation of the congress, the labour of having to
listen to them, maintain them and [90] them is very considerable.

Your Lordship has done me a particular favour in pointing out to me the intrigue of the Duke of Hanover and the
marriage of the Count of [...] with the [?daughter] of the Duchess of Celle, for this person has been [?here] more than two months,
excusing his stay by saying that he could not take service in the war on account of religion. With what Your Lordship
tells me I can cast my nets, for, as I told Your Lordship, I had understood some days ago that Marshal d'Estrades
[?was dealing] with the Duke of Hanover by way of the kinship he has with Madame de [?Arvor]; and [...] that a week
from today I can tell Your Lordship something firm about this plot.

R1001, Ronquillo, 06 Feb 1677

Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen 6 Feb 1677: the plenipotentiary preliminary; Dutch haste and Beverningh; Lira absent in Brussels; talk of a Dutch-Swedish separate peace; an "interim" proposal at the Bremen assembly that will "go up in smoke".

Reading

R1001_reading.md

<!-- Reading of the ciphered passages, built 22 Oct 2026 by Claude Opus 5.5 (ronq batch): the key decode (read/R1001.dec.txt, keyx.py) aligned word by word with the contemporary decipherment in the left margin, transcribed from the DECODE images (DECODE's margin transcription checked against them). Spelling (v6, 23 Oct 2026): the writer's, as the cipher gives it; the modernised spelling of 22 Oct was restored letter by letter from the key decode (read2/R1001.txt) with research/reading_audit/v6_charalign.py: where the key's letters spell the same word another way they are taken (tomavan, assi, devia, aora, quando, haver), editorial accents are taken off, and ñ is kept where the key writes n (it does not distinguish them). Code words, supplied and bracketed words keep their form without accents. No word was changed. Punctuation added. Modern spelling is only in the English file. Words in ⟨ ⟩ are written in clear in the letter and lead into the cipher; [...] = hole; [?word] = uncertain. -->

# R1001, Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen, 6 Feb 1677

⟨... y creyendo que no se ofreceran otros reparos, saldremos muy presto de este preliminar,⟩ a cuyo ajustamiento ha
sido preciso contribuir por nuestra parte por la prisa de holandeses y las voces que an dado a este fin, pasando a
descubrir su animo publicamente, y particularmente Beverningk. Pero es tanvien cierto que, cuando le hablo yo a solas,
le hallo mas sosegado; y todos ellos me dan grandes seguridades y promesas de que no nos avandonaran, sacando para esta
consecuencia la galanteria con que el Principe de Orange ha enviado a Flandes la gente que se le ha pedido. Pero no por
esto se sosiega mi cuidado de que de una manera o de otra nos jueguen alguna pieza, y asi no los dejo de la mano, sin
envargo de alguna noticia que he tenido (de las que puedo adquirir en ausencia de don Manuel de Lira, cuya falta en La
Haya no puedo ponderar a V.S. de cuanto daño e inconveniente nos es) de que los Estados atienden a nuestros intereses y
que conocen les inporta a ellos. Pero como don Manuel de Lira se esta en Bruselas, que es quien podia indagar y
reconocer mas abiertamente el dictamen de holandeses, no se puede dar credito a nada; y asi buelvo a decir a V.S. que
mi mayor cuidado es atender a las acciones de estos embajadores holandeses y caminar junto con ellos.

Hasta aora se habla con fuerza en la paz separada con Suecia, como en mi ultima referi a V.S. distintamente, y le dije
los motivos que tienen holandeses para salir de la guerra, encaminando sus maximas en esto en veneficio nuestro. Pero
yo procuro dar a entender a Beverningk, que es quien mas lo garlea, que de ninguna manera lo puede ser menos que en caso
de que este ajuste intervenga en una paz general; y las razones que alego V.S. las habra visto. En que parara [no se
sabe], ni hasta aora se ha hablado en ello formalmente en el congreso.

Repito a V.S. las gracias por las noticias que me participa de los pasos que se van dando en esa asamblea y las
dificultades que V.S. reconoce en el ajuste de esas materias. Y en lo que V.S. me dice de que se ha hecho abertura de
que se salga de embarazos por via de un interim, a que parece se inclinan daneses, debo decir a V.S. que, segun mis
noticias, me parece que holandeses no vendran en nada que sea ajuste; y que a este fin a venido a La Haya el ministro de
Holanda, y yo creo que sera la ida del humo, porque los Estados quieren mantener las dificultades que ocurren para que
despues quede a su arbitrio la conclusion. V.S., que se halla al pie de la obra, conocera mejor que ninguno lo que ay en
esto, y a mi solo me toca el ponderar el acierto y prudencia con que V.S. discurre, y suplicarle se sirva de continuarme
sus noticias.

## Notes

- The margin decipherment runs beside every cipher line on ff. 2-5 (the cipher starts on f. 2 after "saldremos muy
  presto de este preliminar") and continues under the cipher on ff. 4 and 5 in the decipherer's hand. Read from the
  images in full; DECODE's transcription garbles it ("weminguen", "efrados", "ministro de Holande", "aererzo").
- `120` = cuidado here, twice, against keyx.py's 120 = congreso: f. 3 "se sosiega mi cuidado" (figures `ic 120`, after a
  struck first attempt `ef ef of 120`) and f. 4 "mi mayor cuidado es" (`ic ac ro 31 120 ye`). The margin is clear both
  times. "en el congreso" at the end of f. 4 is spelled out in syllables.
- "Beverningk" (Hieronymus van Beverningh, Dutch plenipotentiary): margin "Berninguen"/"Weminguen", key "por e r ningue n"
  (twice).
- "En qué parará [no se sabe]": neither margin nor figures write a verb; the bracket supplies the sense.
- The first cipher figures on f. 2 (`C+ 49 3 ++ 21`, key "dude de") are not rendered in the margin, which starts "a cuyo
  ajustamiento"; read as nulls/lead-in, not as text.
- "garlea" = garlar, to chatter about it. "la ida del humo" = it will come to nothing (go up in smoke).
- Don Manuel de Lira was the Spanish envoy at The Hague; "los Estados" = the States General.
Translation

R1001_en.md

<!-- English translation of read/R1001_reading.md (Claude Opus 5.5, 22 Oct 2026). Words in ⟨ ⟩ are in clear in the letter. -->

# R1001, Ronquillo to Fuenmayor, Nijmegen, 6 Feb 1677

⟨... and believing that no other objections will be raised, we shall very soon be done with this preliminary,⟩ to the
settlement of which it has been necessary to contribute on our side because of the haste of the Dutch and the clamour
they have raised to this end, going so far as to reveal their mind publicly, and Beverningk in particular. But it is
also true that when I speak to him alone I find him calmer; and all of them give me great assurances and promises that
they will not abandon us, drawing as proof of this the gallantry with which the Prince of Orange has sent to Flanders
the troops that were asked of him. But this does not quiet my concern that in one way or another they may play some
trick on us, and so I do not let go of them, notwithstanding some news I have had (of the kind I can obtain in the
absence of Don Manuel de Lira, whose absence from The Hague I cannot sufficiently convey to Your Lordship, how much harm
and inconvenience it does us) that the States are attending to our interests and know that it matters to themselves.
But as Don Manuel de Lira is staying in Brussels, and he is the one who could find out and discern more openly the mind
of the Dutch, nothing can be believed; and so I say again to Your Lordship that my greatest concern is to watch the
actions of these Dutch ambassadors and to walk together with them.

So far there is strong talk of the separate peace with Sweden, as I related distinctly to Your Lordship in my last, and
I told you the reasons the Dutch have for getting out of the war, directing their policy in this to our benefit. But I
try to make Beverningk, who is the one who talks most about it, understand that it can by no means be so unless this
settlement comes within a general peace; and Your Lordship will have seen the reasons I put forward. How it will end
[is not known], nor has it so far been spoken of formally in the congress.

I repeat my thanks to Your Lordship for the news you give me of the steps being taken in that assembly and of the
difficulties Your Lordship sees in settling those matters. And as to what Your Lordship tells me, that an opening has
been made for getting out of the difficulties by way of an interim, to which the Danes seem inclined, I must tell Your
Lordship that, according to my information, it seems to me that the Dutch will agree to nothing that is a settlement;
and that the minister of Holland has come to The Hague for this purpose, and I believe it will go up in smoke, because
the States want to keep up the difficulties that arise so that the conclusion is afterwards left to their discretion.
Your Lordship, who is on the spot, will know better than anyone what there is in this, and it falls to me only to
praise the judgement and prudence with which Your Lordship reasons, and to beg you to be pleased to continue sending me
your news.

Key: keyx.py
